     (i) Real time processing ;:H ld,Sk ieliqu
     Real-time processing refers to the immediate processing of data as it is input or received, with minimal
     delay. This is crucial in systems where timely responses are essential, such as in online transactions, live
     video streaming, or industrial control systems. Real-time processing ensures that data is processed and the
     system responds almost instantaneously to events.
     ;;H ld,Sk ieliqï hkq wju m%udohlska o;a; wdodkh ,enqKq úg laIkslj ieliSuhs' ine¢ .kqfok

     ieliqï uÕska o;a; ielfik nj iy;sl lrk w;r moaO;sh isÿùï j,g laIkslj m%;spdr olajhs'

     (i) Single User-Single Tasking tAl mßYS,l"-tAl ld¾hh

             tAl mßYS,l (- moaO;sh iuÕ tljr wka;¾l%shd l< yelafla tla mßYS,lfhl=g muKs'
         •   Single Tasking :- Only one task (or process) can run at a time.
             tAl ld¾hh (- jrlg l%shd;aul l< yelafla tla ld¾hhla ^fyda l%shdj,shla& muKs'
         Example :- Early personal computers like MS-DOS systems where the user could run one program at

         mqoa.,sl mß.Kl'

     (ii) Single User, Multi Tasking tAl mßYS,l" nyq ld¾h

         •   Single User :- Only one user can interact with the system at a time.
             ;ks mßYS,l (- moaO;sh iuÕ tljr wka;¾l%shd l< yelafla tla mßYS,lfhl=g muKs'
         •   Multi Tasking :- Multiple tasks (or processes) can run simultaneously.
             nyq ld¾h (- nyq ld¾hhka ^fyda l%shdj,s& tljr l%shd;aul úh yel'
         Example :- Modern desktop operating systems like Windows, macOS, or Linux where a single user

  3. Which of the following lists a computer memory hierarchy in the descending order of access speed?
     mß.Kl u;l OQrdj,shla m%fõY fõ.fha wjfrdayK mÜmdáhg ilid we;af;a my; i|yka ljrl o?
     1)   hard disk, registers, L2 cache, L1 cache, main memory
     2)   main memory, L1 cache, registers, L2 cache, hard disk
     3)   registers, main memory, hard disk, L1 cache, L2 cache
     4)   registers, L1 cache, L2 cache, main memory, hard disk
     5)   L1 cache, L2 cache, registers, main memory, hard disk

     Answer : 4 )
     1. Hard disk oDV ;eáh
     A hard disk drive (HDD) is a data storage device that uses magnetic storage to store and retrieve digital
     information. It consists of one or more rigid rapidly rotating disks (platters) coated with magnetic material.
     The data is read from and written to the disk using a head that moves across the disk's surface.
     oDV ;eáh (HDD) hkq äðg,a f;dr;=re .nvd lsÍu iy ,nd .ekSu i|yd pqïnl wdphkh Ndú;d
     lrk o;a; .nvd lsÍfï WmlrKhls' th pqïnl øjH j,ska wdf,am lrk ,o oDv fõ.fhka N%uKh
     jk ;eá tlla fyda lsysmhlska iukaú; fõ' ;eáfha u;=msg yryd .uka lrk ysila Ndú;fhka o;a;
     lshjd ;eáhg ,shd we;

     2. Registers frðiag¾
     Registers are small, high-speed storage locations within a computer's central processing unit (CPU). They
     are used to hold data temporarily during the execution of instructions.
     frðiag¾ hkq mß.Klfha uOHu ieliqï tAllhla (CPU) ;=< l=vd yd wêfõ.S .nvd ia:dk fõ'
     Wmfoia l%shd;aul lsÍfï§ o;a; ;djld,slj r|jd .ekSug tAjd Ndú;d fõ'

     3. Cache Memory jdrl u;lh
     Cache memory is a small, high-speed memory located close to the CPU. It stores frequently accessed
     data and instructions to reduce the time the CPU takes to retrieve this information from the main
     memory (RAM).
     jdrl u;lh hkq CPU tlg wdikakj msysgd we;s l=vd" wêfõ.S u;lhls' th m%Odk u;lfhka
     (RAM) fuu f;dr;=re ,nd .ekSug CPU tlg .;jk ld,h wvq lsÍug ks;r m%fõY jk o;a;
     iy Wmfoia .nvd lrhs'

     Types of Cache Memory
     jdrl u;lh j¾.

     •    L1 Cache (Level 1 Cache)
          Location:- Closest to the CPU cores.
          ia:dkh(- CPU uOHhg wdikakj'
          Size:- Typically very small, ranging from 16KB to 128KB per core.
          m%udKh( idudkHfhka b;d l=vd" yrhl 16KB isg 128KB olajd mrdihl mj;S'
          Purpose:- Stores the most frequently accessed data and instructions that the CPU needs immediately.
          CPU fj; laIKslj wjYH jk ks;r m%fõY jk o;a; iy Wmfoia .nvd lrhs

     •   L2 Cache (Level 2 Cache)
         Location:- Slightly farther from the CPU cores than L1 cache, but still on the same chip
         ia:dkh(- L1 jdrl u;lh jvd CPU yrfhka u|la ÿßka" kuq;a ;ju;a tlu Ñmfha we;'
         Size:- Larger than L1 cache, ranging from 256KB to a few megabytes per core.
         m%udKh(- L1 jdrl u;lh jvd úYd," yrhlg 256KB isg fu.dnhsÜ lsysmhla olajd mrdihl mj;S'
         Speed:- Slower than L1 cache but faster than L3 cache, with access times typically around 10-20
         nanoseconds.
         fõ.h(- L1 jdrl u;lh jvd ukao.dó kuq;a L3 jdrl u;lhg jvd fõ.j;a" m%fõY fõ,djka
         idudkHfhka kefkda ;;amr 10-20 muK fõ'
         Purpose:- Acts as a secondary cache to L1, storing data and instructions that are not as frequently
         accessed but still require quick retrieval.
         wruqK(- L1 fj; oaú;shsl jdrl u;lh f,i l%shd lrhs" ks;r m%fõY fkdjk kuq;a ;ju;a blauka
         ,nd .ekSu wjYH jk o;a; iy Wmfoia .nvd lrhs'

     •   L3 Cache (Level 3 Cache)
         Location:- Shared among all the cores on a multi-core processor, typically farther from the cores than
         L1 and L2 caches.
         ia:dkh(- laIqø ilik we;s ish¨u yrhka w;r fnodf.k we;" idudkHfhka L1 iy L2 jdrl
         u;lhg jvd uOHhkaf.ka ÿßka'
         Size:- The largest cache, ranging from a few megabytes to tens of megabytes.
         m%udKh(- fu.dnhsÜ lsysmhl isg fu.dnhsÜ oyhla olajd jQ úYd,;u jdrl u;lh
         Speed:- Slower than L1 and L2 caches but faster than main memory, with access times typically
         around 20-50 nanoseconds.
         fõ.h(- L1 iy L2 jdrl u;l jvd ukao.dó kuq;a m%Odk u;lhg jvd fõ.j;a" m%fõY fõ,djka
         idudkHfhka kefkda ;;amr 20-50 muK fõ'
         Purpose:- Serves as a last-level cache before accessing the slower main memory. It helps reduce the
         latency for accessing data that is not found in L1 or L2 caches.
         wruqK(- ukao.dó m%Odk u;lhg m%fõY ùug fmr wjidk uÜgfï jdrl u;lhla f,i fiajh
         lr th L1 fyda L2 jdrl u;l fkdue;s o;a; fj; m%fõY ùfï m%udoh wvq lsÍug WmldÍ fõ'

     4. Main Memory / Random Access Memory (RAM) m%Odk u;lh
     RAM (Random Access Memory) is a type of computer memory that is used to store data and machine
     code currently being used. RAM is volatile, meaning it loses its data when the computer is turned off.
     RAM plays a crucial role in a computer's performance by providing space for the operating system,
     applications, and data that are in use.
     Random Access Memory(RAM) hkq oekg Ndú;d jk o;a; iy hka;% fla; .nvd lsÍug Ndú;d
     lrk mß.Kl u;l j¾.hls' RAM kYH fõ" tkï mß.Klh l%shd úrys; jQ úg tys o;a; ke;s
     ù hhs' fufyhqï moaO;sh" fhÿï iy Ndú;fha mj;sk o;a; i|yd bv ,nd §fuka mß.Klhl
     l%shdldß;ajh i|yd RAM ;SrKd;aul ld¾hNdrhla bgq lrhs'

     In the diagram below, speed and cost increase from bottom to top, while size and capacity increase from
     top to bottom.
     my; rEmigykg wkQj fõ.h iy msßjeh my< isg by<g;a m%udKh iyd Odß;dj by< isg my<g;a
     jeä fõ'

  4. Which of the following gives the correct results of bit-wise AND and bit-wise OR operations between
     the two binary numbers 010101002 and 111010012 respectively?
     01010100" iy 11101001 hk oaúuh ixLHd fol w;r ìgq wkqidß; (bit-wise) AND iy ìgq
     wkqidß; OR fufyhqïj, ksjerÈ m%;sM, ms<sfj<ska oelafjkafka my; ljrl o?
     1)   010000002, 111111012
     2)   000000102, 101110012
     3)   101111012, 110010102
     4)   110000002, 001011002
     5)   111111012, 010100112

     Answer : 1 )
     Bitwise AND
     The result of the bitwise AND operation is a new binary number where each bit is 1 if the corresponding
     bits of both operands are 1, otherwise it is 0.
     ìgq wkqidß; AND fufyhqfï m%;sM,h kj oaúuh ixLHdjla jk w;r tys§ ìgq hq.,hka foflysu
     wkqrEm ìgq folu 1 kï m%;sodk ìgqj 1 fõ" tfia fkdue;s kï th 0 fõ'

     Bitwise OR
     The result of the bitwise OR operation is a new binary number where each bit is 1 if the corresponding bit
     of either or both operands is 1, otherwise it is 0.
     ìgq wkqidß; OR fufyhqfï m%;sM,h kj oaúuh ixLHdjla jk w;r tys§ wkqrEm tla ìgqjla 1 kï

  5. What is the correct binary equivalent of decimal 12.7510?
     oYuh 12.7510 g ;=,H jk ksjerÈ oaùuh ixLHdj l=ula o?

     1) 1011.012           2) 1011.112            3) 1100.002           4) 1100.112            5) 1100.012

     Answer : 4 )
     Following steps can be followed to convert a decimal number with decimals to another base.
     oYu iys; oYuh ixLHdjla fjk;a mdohlg mßj¾;kh lsÍfï§ my; mshjr wkq.ukh l<
     yelsh'

     While converting this number to binary base, we can first consider only the non-decimal part and divide
     by the corresponding base. When the remainder is written in front of the division and the numbers are
     lined up from bottom to top, the corresponding number is obtained.
     fuu ixLHdj oaúuh mdohg mßj¾;kh lsÍfï§ m<uqj fuys oYu rys; fldgi muKla i,ld
     wod< mdofhka fnÈh yelsh' tf,i fn§fï§ ,efnk b;sßh bÈßfhka ,shd my< isg by<g tu
     ixLHd fm<.eiajQ úg wod< ixLHdj ,efí'

     2    12
     2    6     -0
     2    3     -0
     2    1     -1
          0     -1

     Accordingly, tA wkqj,
     1210 = 11002

     Then let's consider the decimal part of this. Here the decimal part should be multiplied by the relevant
     base until it reaches 0. Once a digit reaches the non-decimal part, it should be treated as 0 again in the next
     step. Then the digits coming in the non-decimal part are aligned from top to bottom to get the
     corresponding number. No 0 should be applied to the non-decimal part at the beginning of this.
     miqj fuys oYu fldgi i,ld n,uq' fuys§ oYu fldgi 0 jk f;la wod< mdofhka .=K l< hq;=h'
     oYu rys; fldgig b,lalula meñKs miq th B<Õ mshjf¾§ kej; 0 f,i ie,lsh hq;=h' túg
     oYu rys; fldgig meñfKk b,lalï by< isg my<g fm<.eiajQ úg wod< ixLHdj ,efí' fuys
     wdrïNfha§ oYu rys; fldgig 0 fkdfhÈh hq;=h'

          .75 x 2
         1.50 x 2
         1.00

     Accordingly, tA wkqj,

     .7510 = .112

     Accordingly, the correct number can be written as follows.
     tA wkqj ksjerÈ ixLHdj my; mßÈ ,súh yelsh'

     12.7510 = 1100.112

     1’s complement tflys wkqmQrlh

     changed to 0s.

     fjkia fõ'

     2’s complement foflys wkqmQrlh

     2's complement is a way to represent signed integers in binary, where the most significant bit (MSB)
     represents the sign of the number.
     2 ys wkqmQrlh hkq oaúuh ;=< ,l=Kq iys; ksÅ, ksrEmkh lsÍfï l%uhls" tys§ jeäu fjfiis
     ìgqj (MSB) ixLHdfõ ,l=K ksfhdackh lrhs'

     Signed magnitude method ,l=Kqj;a ixLHd ksrEmKh
     The signed magnitude method is a way to represent signed integers in binary, where the most significant
     bit (MSB) is used to indicate the sign of the number. The remaining bits represent the magnitude of the
     number.
     ,l=Kqj;a ixLHd ksrEmK l%uh hkq oaúuh ;=< ,l=Kqj;a ksÅ, ksrEmKh lsÍfï l%uhls" tys§
     ixLHdfõ ,l=K oelaùug jeäu fjfiis ìgqj (MSB) Ndú;d fõ' b;sß ìgq ixLHdfõ úYd,;ajh
     ksfhdackh lrhs'

     The MSB is used to indicate whether the number is positive (0) or negative (1).
     ixLHdj Ok (0) o iDK (1) o hkak oelaùug MSB Ndú;d fõ'

     +4110 → 001010012
     -4110 → 101010012

     Unsigned magnitude representation ,l=Kq rys; ixLHd ksrEmKh

     The unsigned magnitude representation is a way to represent non-negative integers in binary. In this
     method, the binary digits (bits) are used solely to represent the magnitude or value of the number,
     without any sign information.
     ,l=Kq rys; ixLHd ksrEmKh hkq oaúuh ;=< iDK fkdjk mQ¾K ixLHd ksfhdackh lrk wdldrhls'

     +4110 → 001010012

  11. Consider the following truth table:
      my; i;H;d j.=j i,lkak'

       A      B    C        Z
       0      0    0        0

     Answer : 2, 4 )

     Grey code

     In a Gray code sequence, adjacent values only differ by a single bit. This means that adjacent cells in the
     Karnaugh map also only differ by a single Boolean variable.
     Gray code wkqms<sfj,l" hdno w.hka fjkia jkafka ;ks ìgqjlska muKs' fuhska woyia lrkafka ldfkda
     is;shfï hdno fldaI o fjkia jkafka ;ks nQ,shdkq úp,Hhlska muKla njhs'

     The Karnaugh map is designed to "wrap around" at the edges, treating the map as a 3D cylinder or torus.
     With the first and last values differing by only one bit.
     ldfkda is;shu ;%sudK is,skavrhla fyda fgdaria tlla f,i i,lñka" odrj, “jfÜ t;Sug” ks¾udKh
     lr we;' m<uq iy wjidk w.hka tl ìÜ tllska muKla fjkia fõ'

     Accordingly, considering this Gray code property, answers 1, 3, 5 can be removed.
     tA wkqj fuu Gray code .=Kdx.h i,ld 1, 3, 5 ms<s;=re bj;a l< yelsh'

     Considering answers 2 and 4, it appears that the Gray code of the Karnaugh maps is correct.
     2 iy 4 ms<s;=re ie,l+ úg tu ldfkda is;shï j, Gray code tl ksjerÈ nj fmkS hhs'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -19-                                            #ictfromabc

     The seven-state process model is an extension of the traditional five-state process model in operating
     systems. It provides a more detailed and comprehensive view of process states and transitions. Here are
     the seven states and their explanations:
     ;;aj yf;a l%shdhk rEm igyk hkq fufyhqï moaO;sj, iïm%odhsl ;;aj mfya l%shdhk rEm igyfkau
     È.=jls' fuu.ska l%shdj,s ;;a;ajhka iy ixl%dka;s ms<sn| jvd;a iúia;rd;aul iy mq¿,a oelaula imhhs'
     ;;aj y; iy tAjdfha ld¾hhka fjk fjku my; oelafõ'

     • New kj$ks¾ñ; ;;ajh
       The process is being created.
       l%shdj,sh ks¾udKh fjñka mj;S'
       At this stage, the process is not yet ready to execute. It is in the process of being initialized, including
       allocation of necessary resources.
       fuu wÈhf¾§" l%shdj,sh ;ju;a l%shd;aul lsÍug iQodkï ke;' wjYH iïm;a fjka lsÍu we;=¿j
       th wdrïN lsÍfï l%shdj,shl mj;S'

     • Ready iQodkï ;;ajh
       The process is prepared to run but is waiting for CPU time.
       l%shdj,sh l%shd;aul ùug iQodkï kuq;a uOH ieliqï tAllh ld,h tkf;la n,d isà'
       The process has all the resources it needs except for the CPU. It is placed in the ready queue, waiting
       for the scheduler to select it for execution.
       uOH ieliqï tAllh yer thg wjYH ish¨u iïm;a l%shdj,shg we;' th l%shd;aul lsÍu i|yd
       ld,igykalre úiska f;dard .kakd f;la n,d isák" iQodkï fmda,sfï ;nd we;'

     • Running Odjk ;;ajh
       The process is currently being executed by the CPU.
       fuu l%shdj,sh oekg uOH ieliqï tAllh u.ska l%shd;aul fõ'
       The process is actively using the CPU. Only one process can be in the running state on a single-core
       processor at any given time.
       l%shdj,sh il%shj uOH ieliqï tAllh Ndú;d lrhs' ´kEu wjia:djl ;ks yr ilikhl Odjk
       ;;ajfha mej;sh yelafla tla l%shdj,shla muKs'

     • Blocked (or Waiting) wjysr l< ;;ajh

       l%shdj,sh hï isÿùula isÿ jk f;la n,d isà ^Wod'" wdodk$m%;sodk iïmQ¾K lsÍu" iïm;a ,nd
       .ekSfï yelshdj&'
       The process cannot proceed until the specific event it is waiting for happens. It is not eligible for the
       CPU until it moves back to the ready state.
       th n,d isák ksYaÑ; isÿùu isÿjk f;la l%shdj,sh bÈßhg hd fkdyel' th kej; iQodkï
       ;;a;ajhg f.k hk f;la th uOH ieliqï tAllh fj; hdu i|yd iqÿiq fkdfõ'

     • Suspended (Ready) m%;syrKh l< /§ isák ;;ajh
       The process is in the ready state but has been swapped out of main memory.
       l%shdj,sh iQodkï ;;ajfha mj;sk kuq;a m%Odk u;lfhka udre lr we;'
       This state is used to manage memory more efficiently. The process is ready to execute but is
       temporarily stored on disk.
       u;lh jvd;a ld¾hlaIuj l<ukdlrKh lsÍug fuu ;;ajh Ndú;d fõ' l%shdhkh l%shd;aul
       lsÍug iQodkï kuq;a ;djld,slj ;eáfha .nvd lr we;'

     • Suspended (Blocked) m%;syrKh l< wjysr l< ;;ajh
       The process is in the blocked state and has been swapped out of main memory.
       l%shdj,sh wjysr ù we;s w;r m%Odk u;lfhka udre lr we;'
       Similar to the suspended ready state, this state helps manage memory. The process is waiting for an
       event and is also swapped out of memory.
       w;aysgqjk ,o iQodkï ;;ajhg iudk" fuu ;;ajh u;lh l<ukdlrKh lsÍug WmldÍ fõ'

     • Terminated wjika l< ;;ajh

     Now let's find out the number of categories related to the file step by step.

      FAT
     200     202
     201     200
     202      –1

     204     205

     Here -1 indicates the last category. The last category file is referred to by category number 202.
     fuys -1 ka oelafjkafka wjidk ldKavh hs' wjidk ldKavhg f.dkqj fhduq ù we;af;a 202 jk
     ldKav wxlfhks'

     number.

     wxlfhks'

     201 fj; f.dia neÆúg th fj; fhduq ù we;af;a 203 fjks ldKav wxlfhks'

     The TCP/IP architecture, also known as the Internet Protocol Suite, is a set of communication protocols
     used for the Internet and similar networks.

          o;a; m%jdy md,kh i|yd j.lsj hq;=h'

          in order.

     A. Transmission Control Protocol (TCP)
        iïfm%aIK md,k kshudj,sh

  16. Which of the following statements is/are correct about MAC and IPv4 addresses?

  18. An organization with the assigned IP address block 193.1.1.0/24 needs to define eight subnets. Each

     1) 24, 3, 5           2) 24, 5, 3             3) 24, 27, 5         4) 27, 3, 5         5) 27, 30, 2

     Answer : 3 )
     Let's build the answer step by step.
     mshjfrka mshjr ms<s;=r f.dv k.uq'

     First let's identify the network part and host part of the given address.
     m%:ufhka u § we;s ,smskfha cd, fldgi iy i;aldrl fldgi yÿkd .ksuq'

     Thus, it appears that there are 24 bits for the network part and 8 bits for the host part (because an IPv4
     address consists of 32 bits in total).

     ^IPv4 ,smskhla uq¿ ìgq 32 lska iukaú; ksid&'

     i;aldrl i|yd b;sßj we;s ìgq .Kk ;SrKh lruq'

     i;aldrl i|yd uq,a ìgq 8 ka" wms Wmcd, i|yd ìgq 3 la Ndú;d lruq'túg wmg i;aldrl i|yd
     ìgq 5la b;sß lrhs'
     With 5 bits for the host part, the number of available IP addresses per subnet is 25 = 32.
     i;aldrl fldgi i|yd ìgq 5la iuÕska" Wmcd,hlg ;sfnk IP ,smsk .Kk 25 = 32 fõ'
     However, we need to subtract 2 addresses (network address and broadcast address) which leaves us with

     Wmcd,hlg Ndú; l< yels IP ,smsk 30la wmg b;sß fõ'
     Since 30 usable addresses satisfy the requirement of more than 25 IP addresses per subnet, this works.
     Ndú; l< yels ,smsk 30 ka tla Wmcd,hlg IP ,smsk 25lg jvd wjYH jk neúka" fuh l%shd
     lrhs'
     Accordingly, the correct bit allocation is,
     tA wkqj ksjerÈ ìgq fjkalsÍu jkafka"
     The number of bits required to identify the network is – 24 bits
     cd,h yÿkd.ekSug wjYH ìgq .Kk jkafka - ìgq 24

     backbone. In a network, data travels through this cable in both directions, and every device on the network
     receives the same signal simultaneously.

     Ring Topology uqÿ ia:,lh
     In a ring topology, devices are connected in a closed loop, forming a circular path for data transmission.
     Each device acts as a repeater, receiving data packets, regenerating the signal to maintain its strength, and
     then forwarding it to the next device in the ring.
     uqÿ ia:,lh ;=<" Wmdx. ixjD; Æmhla ;=< iïnkaO lr we;s w;r" o;a; iïfm%aIKh i|yd jD;a;dldr

     is the most inefficient network.

     Mesh topology oe,a ia:,lh
     Mesh topology is a web-like network structure where
     devices are interconnected with each other, as opposed to
     relying on a central hub or switch like in star topology.
     Imagine a fishnet where each knot connects to multiple other
     knots, creating a redundant network of pathways. In a mesh
     network, data can travel along multiple paths between
     devices, providing flexibility and fault tolerance.
     oe,a ia:, úoHdj hkq ;re ia:, úoHdfõ fuka uOHu
     flakaøhla fyda iaúphla u; hemSug m%;súreoaOj Wmdx.

     4)    P – 3, Q – 1, R – 4, S – 2
     5)    P – 4, Q – 2, R – 1, S – 3

     The Seven Layers of the OSI Model
     OSI udÈ,sfha ia;r y;

     1. Physical Layer (Layer 1)
        fN!;sl ia;rh ^m<uq ia;rh&
        This layer is concerned with the physical connection between devices. It deals with the transmission
        and reception of raw binary data over a physical medium such as cables, radio waves, or fiber optics.

     Digital Signature wxls; w;aik

     A digital signature is a cryptographic technique used to ensure the authenticity, integrity, and non-
     repudiation of a digital message or document. It functions similarly to a handwritten signature or a stamped
     seal, but it provides far more inherent security. Digital signatures are commonly used in various digital
     transactions, communications, and data exchanges to verify the identity of the sender and the integrity of
     the message.
     wxls; w;aikla hkq äðg,a mKsúvhl fyda f,aLkhl i;H;dj" wLKav;dj iy m%;slafIam fkdlsÍu
     iy;sl lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d lrk .=ma; f,aLk Ys,amSh l%uhls' th w;ska ,shk ,o w;aiklg fyda uqød
     ;enQ uqødjlg iudkj l%shd lrhs" kuq;a th jvd;a wdfõksl wdrlaIdjla imhhs' hjkakdf.a wkkH;djh
     iy mKsúvfha wLKav;dj ;yjqre lsÍu i|yd úúO äðg,a .kqfokq" ikaksfõokh iy o;a; yqjudre
     j,§ äðg,a w;aika nyq,j Ndú;d fõ'

     Asymmetric Key Encryption wiuñ;sl .=ma; fla;kh
     Asymmetric key encryption, also known as public-key cryptography, is a type of encryption that uses a
     pair of keys for secure communication: a public key and a private key. These keys are mathematically
     related but distinct. The public key is used for encryption, while the private key is used for decryption.
     This system addresses the key distribution problem of symmetric key encryption, where the same key is
     used for both encryption and decryption.
     wiuñ;sl .=ma; fla;kh" fmdÿ h;=re .=ma;fla;kh f,io ye¢kafõ" th wdrlaIs; ikaksfõokh i|yd
     h;=re hq.,hla Ndú;d lrk ixfla;dxlk j¾.hls' tkï fmdÿ h;=rla iy mqoa.,sl h;=rla fú' fuu
     h;=re .Ks;uh jYfhka iïnkaO kuq;a fjkia h' fmdÿ h;=r fla;kh i|yd Ndú;d lrk w;r
     mqoa.,sl h;=r úfla;kh i|yd Ndú;d fõ' fuu moaO;sh fla;kh iy úfla;kh hk folgu tlu
     h;=r Ndú;d lrk iuñ;sl h;=re ixfla;dxlkfha m%Odk fnodyeÍfï .eg¨j úiohs'

     Public Key fmdÿ h;=r
     Can be freely distributed and shared with anyone.
     ´kEu flfkl=g ksoyfia fnod yeßh yel'
     Used to encrypt messages or verify digital signatures.
     mKsúv fla;kh lsÍug fyda wxls; w;aika i;Hdmkh lsÍug Ndú;d lrhs'

     whs;slre úiska ryis.;j iy iqrlaIs;j ;nd we;'
     Used to decrypt messages or create digital signatures.

     Encryption .=ma;fla;kh
     Encryption is the process of converting plaintext (readable data) into ciphertext (an unreadable format) to
     protect it from unauthorized access. This process ensures that only authorized parties can read the data by
     transforming it back into its original form through decryption.
     .=ma;fla;kh hkq wkjir m%fõYhlska wdrlaId lsÍu i|yd ir, fm< ^lshúh yels o;a;& fla;dxl
     fm< ^lshúh fkdyels wdlD;shla& njg mßj¾;kh lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs' fuu l%shdj,sh úfla;kh yryd
     o;a; kej; tys uq,a iajrEmhg mßj¾;kh lsÍfuka n,h,;a md¾Yajhkag muKla lshúh yels nj
     iy;sl lrhs'

     There are 2 ways.

     1. Symmetric Key Encryption

         Uses the same key for both encryption and decryption.
         fla;kh iy úfla;kh hk folgu tlu h;=r Ndú;d lrhs'
     2. Asymmetric Key Encryption
        wiuñ;sl h;=re .=ma;fla;kh
         Uses a pair of keys a public key for encryption and a private key for decryption.
         h;=re hq.,hla Ndú;d lrhs' tkï fla;kh i|yd fmdÿ h;=rla iy úfla;kh i|yd mqoa.,sl
         h;=rla fõ'

     Decryption úfla;kh

     Decryption is the process of converting ciphertext (encrypted data) back into its original form, known as
     plaintext. This process allows authorized users to access and read the original data. Decryption uses a
     specific algorithm and a key to transform the ciphertext back into readable data.
     úfla;kh hkq fla;dxl fm< ^.=ma;fla;kh lrk ,o o;a;& kej; tys uq,a iajrEmhg mßj¾;kh
     lsÍfï l%shdj,shhs" th ir, fm< f,i ye¢kafõ' fuu l%shdj,sh n,h,;a mßYS,lhskag uq,a o;a; fj;

     úfYaIs; we,af.dß;uhla iy h;=rla Ndú;d lrhs'

     There are 2 ways.

     Plaintext ir, fm<
     Plaintext refers to the original, readable form of data or information before it has been encrypted. This is
     the data that is understandable and can be directly read and interpreted by humans or computers without
     needing any decryption. In the context of cryptography and data security, plaintext is the starting point for
     encryption and the end result of decryption.
     ir, fm< hkq .=ma;fla;kh lsÍug fmr o;a; fyda f;dr;=re j, uq,a" lshúh yels wdldrhhs' fuh
     f;areï .; yels o;a; jk w;r lsisÿ úfla;khla wjYH fkdù ñksiqkag fyda mß.Klj,g fl,skau
     lshjd w¾:l:kh l< yelsh' .=ma;fla;kh iy o;a; wdrlaIK ikao¾Nh ;=<" ir, fm< hkq
     fla;kh i|yd wdrïNl ,laIHh iy úfla;kfha wjidk m%;sM,hhs'

     Ciphertext fla;sl fm<
     Ciphertext is the result of encryption performed on plaintext using an encryption algorithm and a key. This
     transformed data is not readable or understandable without the appropriate decryption key and process.
     Ciphertext ensures that the information is secure and protected from unauthorized access during storage
     or transmission.
     fla;sl fm< hkq fla;dxlk we,af.dß;uhla iy h;=rla Ndú;fhka ir, fm< u; isÿ lrk ,o
     fla;khl m%;sM,hls' fhda.H úfla;k h;=r iy l%shdj,sh fkdue;sj fuu mßj¾;kh lrk ,o o;a;
     lshúh yels fyda f;areï .; fkdyel' .nvd lsÍfï§ fyda iïfm%aIKh lsÍfï§ f;dr;=re iqrlaIs; nj
     iy wkjirfhka m%fõY ùfuka wdrlaId ùu fla;sl fm< iy;sl lrhs'

     3) P – 3, Q – 2, R – 1, S – 5, T – 4

     Data communication protocols are sets of rules and conventions that govern the exchange of data between
     devices over a network. These protocols ensure that data is transmitted accurately, efficiently, and securely.
     Here are some key data communication protocols categorized by the OSI model layers they operate in:
     o;a; ikaksfõok kshudj,s hkq cd,hla yryd Wmdx. w;r o;a; yqjudrej md,kh lrk Í;s iy
     iïuq;Ska iuQyhls' fuu kshudj,s o;a; ksjerÈj" ld¾hlaIuj iy wdrlaIs;j iïfm%aIKh jk nj
     iy;sl lrhs' tAjd l%shd;aul jk OSI wdlD;s ia:r u.ska j¾.SlrKh lrk ,o m%Odk o;a; ikaksfõok
     kshudj,s lsysmhla fufiah

     1. Application Layer Protocols (Layer 7) fhÿï ia:r kshudj,s ^7 jk ia:rh&

         HTTP (Hypertext Transfer Protocol)
             Purpose : Used for transmitting web pages over the Internet.
             wruqK ( wka;¾cd,h yryd fjí msgq iïfm%aIKh lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d fõ'

             Ex : Port: 80 (HTTP), 443 (HTTPS for secure communication).

         FTP (File Transfer Protocol)
             Purpose : Used for transferring files between systems.
             wruqK ( moaO;s w;r f.dkq udre lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d fõ'
             Ex : Port: 21

         SMTP (Simple Mail Transfer Protocol)
             Purpose : Used for sending emails.
             wruqK ( úoHq;a ;eme,a heùug Ndú;d lrhs'
             Ex : Port: 25, 587 (submission), 465 (secure).

         DNS (Domain Name System)
            Purpose : Translates domain names to IP addresses.
            wruqK ( jiï kdu ^IP& ,smsk j,g mßj¾;kh lrhs'
            Ex : Port: 53.

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                 -43-                              #ictfromabc

     2. Transport Layer Protocols (Layer 4) m%jdyk ia:r kshudj,sh ^4 jk ia:rh&

        TCP (Transmission Control Protocol)
           Purpose : Provides reliable, ordered, and error-checked delivery of data.
           wruqK ( úYajikSh" weKjqï l< iy fodaI iys; o;a; fnod yeÍu imhhs'
           Ex : Port : Variable (e.g., 80 for HTTP, 443 for HTTPS).

        UDP (User Datagram Protocol)
           Purpose : Provides a connectionless, fast, but less reliable data transfer.
           wruqK ( iïnkaO;d rys;" fõ.j;a" kuq;a wvq úYajdiodhl o;a; yqjudrejla imhhs'
           Ex : Port : Variable (e.g., 53 for DNS, 67 and 68 for DHCP).

     3. Network Layer Protocols (Layer 3) cd, ia:r kshudj,sh ^3 jk ia:rh&

        IP (Internet Protocol)
           Purpose : Routes packets across network boundaries.
           wruqK ( cd, iSudjka yryd melÜ .uka lrhs'

           Types : IPv4 (32-bit addresses), IPv6 (128-bit addresses).

        ICMP (Internet Control Message Protocol)
           Purpose : Used for error reporting and diagnostic functions.
           wruqK ( fodaI jd¾;d lsÍu iy frda. úksYaph ld¾hhka i|yd Ndú;d fõ'
           Port : No specific port; operates as part of IP.

     4. Data Link Layer Protocols (Layer 2) o;a; iïnkaOl ia:r kshudj,sh ^2 jk ia:rh&
        Ethernet
            Purpose : Facilitates communication within a local area network (LAN).
            wruqK ( m%dfoaYSh cd,hla ^LAN& ;=< ikaksfõokh myiq lrhs'
            Standard : IEEE 802.3.

        PPP (Point-to-Point Protocol)
            Purpose : Used for direct communication between two network nodes.
            wruqK ( cd, fkdavq folla w;r iDcq ikaksfõokh i|yd Ndú;d fõ'
            Standard : RFC 1661.

  25. Which of the following statements is/are correct regarding System Development Life Cycle (SDLC)

      moaO;s ixj¾Ok Ôjk pl% (SDLC) wdlD;s iïnkaOfhka my; l=uk m%ldY$h ksjerÈ o?

     System Development Life Cycle
     moaO;s ixj¾Ok Ôjk pl%h
     The System Development Life Cycle (SDLC) is a framework used to guide the process of developing
     information systems and software applications. There are various SDLC models, each with its own
     approach to organizing and managing these phases.
     moaO;s ixj¾Ok Ôjk pl%h (SDLC) hkq f;dr;=re moaO;s iy uDÿldx. fhÿï ixj¾Okh lsÍfï
     l%shdj,sh fufyhùu i|yd Ndú;d lrk rduqjls' úúO SDLC udÈ,s we;" iEu tllau fuu wÈhrhka
     ixúOdkh lsÍu iy l<ukdlrKh lsÍu i|yd ;ukaf.au m%fõYhla we;'

     Waterfall Model Èh we,s wdlD;sh
     The Waterfall Model is a linear and sequential approach to software development. Each phase must be
     completed before the next one begins, and there is little to no overlap between phases.
     Èh we,s wdlD;sh hkq uDÿldx. ixj¾Okh i|yd f¾Çh iy wkql%ñl m%fõYhls' iEu wÈhrlau B<Õ
     wÈhr wdrïN ùug fmr iïmQ¾K l< hq;= w;r" wÈhr w;r w;sÉPdokh ùu wvqh'

     Phases wÈhr
     1. Requirements Analysis wjYH;d úYaf,aIKh
     2. System Design moaO;s ks¾udKh
     3. Implementation (Coding) l%shd;aul lsÍu ^fla;SlrKh&
     4. Integration and Testing tAldnoaO lsÍu iy mÍlaId lsÍu
     5. Deployment fhoùu
     6. Maintenance kv;a;= lsÍu

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                            -52-                                      #ictfromabc

     Advantages jdis
     •   Simple and easy to understand and use.
         ir, iy f;areï .ekSug iy Ndú;d lsÍug myiqh'
     •   Clearly defined stages.
         meyeÈ,sj ks¾jpkh lrk ,o wÈhr'
     •   Works well for smaller projects with well-defined requirements.
         fyd¢ka w¾: olajd we;s wjYH;d iys; l=vd jHdmD;s i|yd fyd¢ka l%shd lrhs'

     Disadvantages wjdis

      Answer : 2 )
      Functional Requirements ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d
      Functional requirements specify the functions, capabilities, and behaviors that the system must perform to
      satisfy its users and stakeholders. These requirements focus on the specific tasks and activities that the
      system needs to accomplish, often in response to inputs from users or other systems.
      ld¾hnoaO wjYH;d moaO;shla mßYS,lhska iy md¾Yjlrejka ;Dma;su;a lsÍu i|yd moaO;sh úiska
      bgq l< hq;= ld¾hhka" yelshdjka iy yeisÍï Tjqka kshu lrhs' fuu wjYH;d moaO;shg bgq lsÍug
      wjYH ksYaÑ; ld¾hhka iy l%shdldrlï flfrys wjOdkh fhduq lrhs" fndfyda úg mßYS,lhska fyda
      fjk;a moaO;s j,ska ,efnk wdodk j,g m%;spdr olajhs'

      Non Functional Requirements ld¾hnoaO fkdjk wjYH;d
      Non functional requirements specify the attributes and qualities that a system must have, rather than
      specific behaviors or functions it must perform. These requirements describe how well the system
      performs certain functions, rather than what it does. They are crucial for defining the overall quality,
      performance, and usability characteristics of the system.
      ld¾hnoaO fkdjk wjYH;d uÕska moaO;shla bgq l< hq;= ksYaÑ; yeisÍï fyda ld¾hhka fjkqjg ;sìh
      hq;= .=Kdx. kshu lrhs' fuu wjYH;d moaO;sh th lrk foag jvd hï hï ld¾hhka bgq lrkafka
      flfiao hkak úia;r lrhs' moaO;sfha iuia; .=Kd;aulNdjh" ld¾h idOkh iy Wmfhda.S;d ,laIK
      ks¾jpkh lsÍu i|yd tAjd b;d jeo.;a fõ"

      1. The email system should allow users to attach files.
         úoHq;a ;eme,a moaO;sh mßYS,lhskag f.dkq weñKSug bv ,ndÈh hq;= h'
          This statement describes a specific function or capability that the email system should have. It
          specifies what the system should do rather than a quality attribute of how well it should perform.
          fuu m%ldYh úoHq;a ;eme,a moaO;shg ;sìh hq;= ksYaÑ; ld¾hhla fyda yelshdjla úia;r lrhs'

          foa th i|yka lrhs'

  27. Which of the following Data Flow Diagrams (DFDs) is correct with respect to the rules of data flow
     modelling? (Note: A – an external entity, B – a process, C – a data store)
     o;a; .e,Sfï wdlD;slrKh ms<sn| kS;sÍ;s wkqj my; i|yka l=uk o;a; .e,Sï rEigyk (DFD)
     ksjerÈ jkafka o? (igyk ( A – ndysr N=;d¾:hla" B – l%shdj,shla" C – o;a; .nvdjla)

                         1.0                                     1.0
     1)       A                               C           2)                        A               C
                                B                                       B

                          1.0                                    1.0
     3)      A                                C           4)                        A               C
                                B                                       B

     5)       A                                C

     Answer : 1 )
     Let's analyze each option based on the general rules of data flow diagrams:
     o;a; m%jdy rEm igyka j, idudkH Í;s u; mokïj tla tla úl,amh úYaf,aIKh lruq'

     1. Data flow diagrams must have properly directed flows:
        o;a; .e,Sï rEmigykaj,g ksis f,i fhduq l< .e,Sï ;sìh hq;=h(
          Data flows from external entities to processes, from processes to data stores, and from data stores
          back to processes.
          o;a; ndysr N+;d¾: j,ska l%shdj,s j,g" l%shdj,s j,ska o;a; .nvd j,g iy o;a; .nvdfjka
          kej; l%shdj,s j,g .,d hhs'
          Data cannot flow directly between external entities and data stores without going through a process.
          l%shdj,shla yryd fkdf.dia ndysr N+;d¾: iy o;a; .nvd w;r o;a; fl,skau .,d hd fkdyel'

     2. Each component's role:
        tla tla ixrplfha ld¾hNdrh(
          A (external entity): Source or destination of data outside the system.
          A ^ndysr N+;d¾:h&( moaO;sfhka msg; o;a;j, uQ,dY%h fyda .ukdka;h'
          B (process): Transforms incoming data flows into outgoing data flows.
          B ^l%shdj,sh&( tk o;a; m%jdy msg;g hk o;a; m%jdy njg mßj¾;kh lrhs'
          C (data store): Stores data for the system; only processes can interact directly with data stores.
          C ^o;a; .nvdj&( moaO;sh i|yd o;a; .nvd lrhs; o;a; .nvd iuÕ iDcqj wka;¾ l%shd l<
          yelafla l%shdj,Skag muKs'

     Now, let's evaluate each DFD.
     oeka" wms tla tla o;a; .e,Sï rEmigyka we.hSug ,la lruq'

     1) Data flows from external entity A to process B and then to data store C. This aligns with the rules,
        where an external entity sends data to a process, which then can store it in a data store.
        o;a; A isg B ieliSug iy C o;a; .nvdj fj; .,d hhs' fuh kS;sÍ;s iuÕ iumd; fõ" tys§
        ndysr N+;d¾:hla l%shdj,shlg o;a; hjk w;r" th o;a; .nvdjl .nvd l< yel'

     2) Process B sends data to external entity A, and then A sends data to data store C. This is incorrect
        because an external entity should not send data directly to a data store.
        B l%shdj,s A ndysr N+;d¾:hg o;a; hjhs" miqj A o;a; .nvdj C fj; o;a; hjhs' ndysr N+;d¾:hla
        o;a; .nvdjlg fl,skau o;a; fkdheúh hq;= ksid fuh jerÈh'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -59-                                           #ictfromabc

     3) Data flows from external entity A to process B and then to data store C, with an additional incorrect
        flow directly from A back to itself. Also, the arrows between B and C suggest two-way interaction,
        which is unusual for a basic DFD.
        A isg iDcqju kej; tA fj;g wu;r jerÈ .e,Sula iuÕska" B ieliSug iy miqj o;a; .nvdj
        C fj;g A ndysr N+;d¾:fhka o;a; .,d hhs' tfiau" B iy C w;r B;, oaú-ud¾. wka;¾l%shd
        fhdackd lrhs" th uQ,sl DFD i|yd widudkH fõ'
     4) Data flows from process B to external entity A, and then A to data store C. This is incorrect for the
        same reason as (2), where an external entity directly interacts with a data store.
        o;a; m%jdyh B l%shdj,sfha isg A ndysr N+;d¾:h fj;" miqj A isg C o;a; .nvdj olajd .uka
        lrhs fuh jerÈhs (2) jeks fya;=j ksidu" ndysr N+;d¾:hla o;a; .nvdjla iuÕ iDcqj
        wka;¾l%shd lrhs'
     5) Shows data flowing from an external entity A directly to a data store C, which is incorrect as it
        bypasses any processing.
        (ndysr N+;d¾:hlska A fj;ska iDcqju o;a; .nvdjlg C o;a; .,d hk o;a; fmkajhs" th
        lsishï ieliqï uÕyßk neúka th jerÈh'
     Based on these analyses, Diagram 1) is the only diagram that correctly follows the rules of data flow
     modeling.
     fuu úYaf,aIKhka u; mokïj" o;a; .e,Sï rEmigyka kS;s ksjerÈj wkq.ukh lrk tlu rEm

  28. Which of the following statements is correct regarding software testing?
      uDÿldx. mÍlaIdj iïnkaOfhka my; i|yka m%ldY w;=frka ksjerÈ jkafka l=ula o?
     1) Integration testing is usually carried out before unit testing.
        taldnoaO (integration) mÍlaIdj idudkHfhka tall (unit) mÍlaIdjg fmr isÿ flf¾'
     2) Black-box testing techniques are usually used in acceptance testing.
        ld,uxcqid (black-box) mßlaIdfõ Ys,amSh l%u idudkHfhka m%;s.%yK (acceptance) mßlaIdfõ§
        Ndú; fõ'
     3) White box testing examines the behaviour of a software based only on the inputs to a system.
        Yafõ; uxcqid (white-box) mÍlaIdfõ § uDÿldx.hl yeisÍu" moaO;shg ,nd fok wdodk u;
        muKla mokïj mÍlaId flf¾'
     4) Unit testing examines the entire system’s functionality as a whole.
        tall mÍlaIdfõ§ iïmQ¾K moaO;sfha l%shdldÍ;ajh" iuia;hla f,i mÍlaId flf¾'
     5) System testing is usually carried out after the user acceptance testing.
        moaO;s (system) mÍlaIdj idudkHfhka fiajdodhl m%;s.%yK (user acceptance) mÍlaIdjg miqj
        isÿ flf¾'

     Answer : 2 )
     The question lists five statements about software testing and asks which one is correct. Let's analyze each
     statement:
     m%Yakh uDÿldx. mÍlaIdj ms<sn| m%ldY myla ,ehsia;=.; lr we;s w;r l=uk tl ksjerÈ oehs tla
     tla m%ldYh f.k úYaf,aIKh lruq'

     1. Integration testing is usually carried out before unit testing.
        taldnoaO (integration) mÍlaIdj idudkHfhka tall (unit) mÍlaIdjg fmr isÿ flf¾'
         This is incorrect. Unit testing is done first, where individual units or components of a software are
         tested. Integration testing follows unit testing and focuses on the integration of these units to ensure
         they work together as expected.
         fuh jerÈhs' uDÿldx.hl ;ks tAll fyda ixrpl mÍlaId flfrk ;ekska tAll (unit) mÍlaIKh
         m<uqj isÿ flf¾' tAldnoaO;d (integration) mÍlaIKh u.ska tAll (unit) mÍlaIdfjka miq jk w;r

     2. Black-box testing techniques are usually used in acceptance testing.
        ld,uxcqid (black-box) mßlaIdfõ Ys,amSh l%u idudkHfhka m%;s.%yK mßlaIdfõ§ Ndú; fõ'
        This statement is correct. Black-box testing involves testing the software without any knowledge of
        the internal workings of the application. This technique is typically used in acceptance testing where
        the focus is on the functionality according to the requirements, rather than the internal system logic.
        fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈhs' ld,uxcqid (black-box) mÍlaIdj hkq fhÿfï wNHka;r l%shdldß;ajh ms<sn|

 30. Which of the following shows the most suitable primary keys for Order and Order_Product relations?
     Order iy Order_Product j.= i|yd jvd;a iqÿiq m%d:ñl h;=re ms<sfj<ska oelafjkafka my; ljrl o?

      1)   Order: CusId,                Order_ Product: OrderId
      2)   Order: OrderId,              Order_Product: OrderId
      3)   Order: OrderId,              Order_Product: OrderId + ProdId
      4)   Order: CusId + SellerId,     Order Product: ProdId
      5)   Order: OrderId + CusId,      Order_Product: OrderId

      Answer : 3 )

      1. Order Table contains OrderId, CusId, OrderDate, and SellerId.
         Order j.=fõ OrderId, CusId, OrderDate, iy SellerId wvx.= fõ
           OrderId should uniquely identify each record since each order should have a unique identifier.
           iEu weKjqulgu wkkH y÷kd.ekSula ;sìh hq;= neúka OrderId iEu jd¾;djlau wkkH f,i
           y÷kd.; hq;=h'

      2. Order_Product Table is a junction table that connects Order and Product tables and contains
         OrderId and ProdId.
         Order_Product Table hkq weKjqï iy ksIamdok j.= iïnkaO lrk ikaê j.=jla jk w;r
         OrderId iy ProdId wvx.= fõ'
           A combination of OrderId and ProdId is typically used to uniquely identify each record in a
           junction table like this because it defines which products are associated with which orders. A single
           order can contain multiple different products, and the same product can appear in multiple orders.
           OrderId iy ProdId ixfhda.hla idudkHfhka fujeks ikaê j.=jl tla tla jd¾;dj wkkH f,i
           y÷kd .ekSug Ndú;d lrkqfha th l=uk weKjqï iuÕ iïnkaO ù we;ao hkak ks¾jpkh lrk

           weKjqïj, Èia úh yel'

     Given the options:
     ,nd § we;s úl,am j,ska(

     1) Order: CusId, Order_Product: OrderId
        Incorrect because CusId does not uniquely identify an order.
        CusId wkqms<sfj,la wkkH f,i y÷kd fkd.kakd ksid jerÈhs'

     2) Order: OrderId, Order_Product: OrderId

        in Order_Product.

  32. Which of the following is correct regarding the Order relation?
      Order j.=j ie,lSfï§ my; i|yka l=ula ksjerÈ o?
     1) CusId attribute uniquely identifies each tuple in the relation.
        CusId Wm,laIKh (attribute) u.ska j.=fõ tla tla Wm,elshdk (tuple) wkkHj y÷kd.kS'
     2) The relation is in First Normal Form (1NF).
        j.=j tys m%:u m%u; wjia:dfõ (First Normal Form-1NF) mj;S'
     3) The relation is in Second Normal Form (2NF).
        j.=j tys fojk m%u; wjia:dfõ (Second Normal Form-2NF) mj;S'
     4) Orders of each customer are handled by a unique salesperson.
        tla tla mdßfNda.slhdf.a weKjqï yiqrejkq ,nkafka wkkH úl=Kqïlrefjl= úisks'
     5) The relation consists of a composite primary key.
        j.=j ixhqla; m%d:ñl h;=rlska (composite primary key) iukaú; fõ'

     Answer : 3 )

     To determine if a table is in Second Normal Form, it must first meet the criteria for First Normal Form
     (1NF), and then it must satisfy an additional condition related to the way attributes depend on the key.
     j.=jla fojk idudkH fmdarufha ;sfí oehs ;SrKh lsÍu i|yd" th m%:ufhka m<uq idudkH fmdaruh
     (1NF) i|yd jk ks¾Kdhl imqrd,sh hq;= w;r" miqj th úfYaI .=Kdx. u; r|d mj;sk wdldrh
     iïnkaO wu;r fldkafoaishla imqrd,sh hq;=h'

     Criteria for 1NF include:
     1NF i|yd jk ks¾Kdhlj,g we;=<;a jkafka(
     •   No repeating groups or arrays: The table should only have atomic column values.
         mqkrdj¾;k lKavdhï fyda arrays fkdue;( j.=fõ wxY=uh ;Sre w.hka muKla ;sìh hq;=h'
     •   Unique rows: Each row should be uniquely identifiable.
         tAlSh fma<s( iEu fma<shlau wkkHj y÷kd.; yels úh hq;=h'

     Criteria for 2NF include:
     2NF i|yd jk ks¾Kdhlj,g we;=<;a jkafka(
     Elimination of partial dependency: Non-key attributes must depend on the whole of a primary key, not
     just part of a composite key. This means that in a table with a composite key, every non-key attribute
     should depend on all components of the key for its existence.
     wdxYsl mrdh;a;;dj bj;a lsÍu( Non-key attributes fkdjk .=Kdx. ixhqla; h;=rl fldgila muKla
     fkdj m%d:ñl h;=rl iuia;h u; r|d mej;sh hq;=h' fuhska woyia lrkafka ixhqla; h;=rla iys;
     j.=jl" iEu h;=re fkdjk .=Kdx.hlau tys meje;au i|yd h;=f¾ ish¨u ixrpl u; r|d mej;sh
     hq;= njhs'

     Analyzing the Order Table Schema
     Order Table úYaf,aIKh lsÍu
     The schema for the Order table, based on your descriptions, includes the following fields:
     úia;r u; mokïj" Order table i|yd my; lafIa;% we;=<;a fõ(

     OrderId         CusId           OrderDate        SellerId

     Step 1: Verify 1NF
     mshjr 1: 1NF ;yjqre lrkak
     Given that each field in the Order table contains only atomic values (single values in each column per
     row), and each row is uniquely identifiable (presumably by OrderId), the table meets the criteria for 1NF.
     weKjqï j.=fõ iEu lafIa;%hlu wvx.= jkafka mrudKql w.hka ^fma<shlg tla tla ;Srefõ ;ks
     w.hka&" iy tla tla fma<sh wkkHj y÷kd.; yels ^wkqudk jYfhka OrderId u.ska&" j.=j 1NF
     i|yd jk ks¾Kdhl imqrd,hs'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -67-                                          #ictfromabc

     Step 2: Establish the Primary Key
     mshjr ^2(& m%d:ñl h;=r ia:dmkh lrkak
     For 2NF analysis, we need to confirm the primary key. If OrderId is the primary key and uniquely
     identifies each record, this simplifies our analysis.
     2NF úYaf,aIKh i|yd" wms m%d:ñl h;=r ;yjqre l< hq;=h' OrderId m%d:ñl h;=r jk w;r iEu
     jd¾;djlau wkkH f,i y÷kd .kafka kï" fuh wmf.a úYaf,aIKh ir, lrhs'

     Step 3: Check for Partial Dependencies
     mshjr ^3(& wdxYsl mrdh;a;;dj i|yd mÍlaId lrkak
     To be in 2NF:
     2NF ys isàug(
     •   Each attribute in the table should fully depend on the primary key.
         j.=fõ we;s iEu .=Kdx.hlau m%d:ñl h;=r u; iïmQ¾Kfhkau r|d mej;sh hq;=h'
     •   There should be no attribute that depends only on part of the primary key in cases of composite keys.
         ixhqla; h;=re wjia:d j,§ m%d:ñl h;=f¾ fldgila u; muKla r|d mj;sk .=Kdx.hla fkd;sìh
         hq;=h'

     Dependency Analysis:
     mrdh;a;;d úYaf,aIKh(
     •   CusId: This field depends on OrderId as each order can be linked to one customer. The customer of
         an order cannot be determined partially by another subset; it’s entirely dependent on knowing the full
         OrderId.
         CusId: iEu weKjqulau tla mdßfNda.slfhl=g iïnkaO l< yels neúka fuu lafIa;%h OrderId u;
         r|d mj;S' weKjqul mdßfNda.slhd fjk;a Wm l=,lhlska w¾O jYfhka ;SrKh l< fkdyel' th
         iïmQ¾Kfhkau r|d mj;skafka iïmQ¾K OrderId oek .ekSu u; h'
     •   OrderDate and SellerId: Similarly, the date an order was placed and the seller handling the order
         depend entirely on the OrderId. They are not determined by any subset of the OrderId or any other
         field.
         OrderDate and SellerId: tA yd iudkj" weKjqula ,nd ÿka Èkh iy weKjqu yiqrejk úl=Kqïlre
         iïmQ¾Kfhkau OrderId u; r|d mj;S' tAjd OrderId ys Wm l=,lhlska fyda fjk;a lafIa;%hlska
         ;SrKh fkdfõ'

     Conclusion for 2NF
     2NF i|yd ks.ukh
     The Order table is in 2NF because:
     weKjqï j.=j 2NF ys we;s ksid(
     •   It is already in 1NF.
         th oekgu;a 1NF ys we;'
     •   It has a single primary key (OrderId), under which all other fields (non-key attributes) in the table
         show full dependency and no partial dependency exists. The non-key attributes do not depend on
         anything less than the full OrderId.
         thg ;ks m%d:ñl h;=rla (OrderId), we;" tA hgf;a j.=fõ we;s wfkl=;a ish¨u lafIa;% ^m%Odk-
         fkdjk .=Kdx.& mQ¾K mrdh;a;;dj fmkakqï lrk w;r wdxYsl mrdh;a;;djhla fkdue;' m%Odk
         fkdjk .=Kdx. iïmQ¾K OrderId j,g jvd wvq lsisjla u; r|d fkdmj;S'

     3) The relation is in Second Normal Form (2NF) is the correct answer. This is because the Order table
     is already in First Normal Form (1NF) and all non-key attributes (CusId, OrderDate, SellerId) are fully
     dependent on the primary key (OrderId), ensuring no partial dependency exists. Thus, it meets the criteria
     for 2NF.
     3) j.=j tys fojk m%u; wjia:dfõ (Second Normal Form-2NF) mj;S' ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ' ulaksido
     h;a" weKjqï j.=j oekgu;a m<uq idudkH wjia:dfú (1NF) we;s w;r ish¨u m%Odk fkdjk .=Kdx.

  33. Which of the following statements is/are correct regarding the normalization concept?
      m%u;lrKh(normalization) ixl,amh iïnkaOfhka my; i|yka l=uk m%ldY$h ksjerÈ o?

     A - In first normal form, atomic attributes are removed from a relation.
         m<uq m%u; wjia:dfõ§ (1NF), j.=j ;=< we;s mrudKql Wm,laIK (atomic attribute) bj;a lrkq
         ,efí'
     B - In second normal form, partial dependency of attributes on the primary key are removed.
         fojk m%u; wjia:dfõ§ (2NF), m%d:ñl h;=r (primary key) u; Wm,laIKj, wdxYsl mrdh;a;;dj
         (partial dependency) bj;a lrkq ,efí'
     C - In third normal form, transitive dependency of attributes are removed.
         f;jk m%u; wjia:dfõ§ (3NF), Wm,laIKj, ixl%dka;s mrdh;a;;dj (transitive dependency)
         bj;a lrkq ,efí'

     1) B only.        2) A and B only.      3) A and C only.      4) B and C only.      5) All of the above.
        B muKs.           A yd B muKs.          A yd C muKs.          B yd C muKs.          by; ish,a,u.

     Answer : 4 )
     A. In first normal form, atomic attributes are removed from a relation.
        m<uq m%u;lrKfhka" iïnkaO;djhlska wxY=uh .=Kdx. bj;a lrkq ,efí'
         This statement is incorrect as phrased. In first normal form (1NF), a table is in 1NF if it contains only
         atomic values, meaning each column in a table must contain indivisible values. It doesn't involve
         "removing atomic attributes".
         fuu m%ldYh jdlH LKavh f,i jerÈh' m<uq m%u;lrKfhka(1NF), j.=jla wxY=uh w.hka
         muKla wvx.= kï 1NF ys we;" tkï j.=jl we;s iEu ;Srejlu fkdfnÈh hq;= w.hka wvx.=
         úh hq;=h' thg “mrudKql .=Kdx. bj;a lsÍu” we;=<;a fkdfõ'
     B. In second normal form, partial dependency of attributes on the primary key are removed.
        fojk m%u;lrKfhka" m%d:ñl h;=r u; .=Kdx.j, w¾O hemSu bj;a lrkq ,efí'
         This statement is correct. A table is in second normal form (2NF) if it is in 1NF and all non-key
         attributes are fully functionally dependent on the primary key. This means removing any partial
         dependencies where some attributes depend only on part of a composite primary key.
         fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈhs' j.=jla 1NF ys ;sfí kï fojk m%u;lrKfhka (2NF) jk w;r ish¨u
         h;=re fkdjk .=Kdx. m%d:ñl h;=r u; iïmQ¾Kfhkau l%shdldÍj r|d mj;S' fuhska woyia lrkafka
         ixhqla; m%d:ñl h;=rl fldgila u; muKla r|d mj;sk iuyr .=Kdx. ´kEu wdxYsl mrdh;a;;d
         bj;a lsÍuhs'
     C. In third normal form, transitive dependency of attributes are removed.
        f;jk m%u;lrKfhka" .=Kdx.j, ixl%dka;s hemSu bj;a lrkq ,efí'
         This statement is also correct. A table is in third normal form (3NF) if it is in 2NF and no non-key
         attribute depends on other non-key attributes. In other words, it removes transitive dependencies where
         changes to non-key attributes could lead to inconsistencies.
         fï m%ldYh;a ksjerÈhs' j.=jla 2NF ys ;sfí kï ;=kajk m%u;lrKfhka (3NF) jk w;r lsisÿ
         h;=re-fkdjk (non-key) .=Kdx.hla fjk;a h;=re fkdjk(non-key) .=Kdx. u; r|d mj;S' fjk;a
         jpk j,ska lsjfyd;a" th m%Odk fkdjk .=Kdx.j, fjkiaùï fkd.e,mSï j,g ;=vq Èh yels
         ixl%dka;s mrdh;a;;d bj;a lrhs'

     tla tla m%ldYh ksjerÈj úYaf,aIKh lsÍfuka(

             A jerÈhs'
         •   B and C are correct.
             B iy C ksjerÈh
     So, the correct answer is 4).

     N=;d¾: iïnkaO;d wdlD;slrKh(ER Modelling) iïnkaOfhka my; i|yka l=uk m%ldY$h ksjerÈ o?

     To address this question, we will evaluate each statement about Entity Relationship (ER) modeling to
     determine which are correct.
     fuu .eg¨j úi£u i|yd" wms N+;d¾: iïnkaO;d (ER) wdlD;s ks¾udKh ms<sn| tla tla m%ldYh
     ksjerÈ oehs ;SrKh lrkafkuq'

     A - A weak entity is dependent on another entity.
         ÿ¾j, N+;d¾;hla fjk;a N+;d¾:hla u; r|d mj;S'
         Correct. A weak entity does not have a sufficient set of attributes to form a primary key on its own and
         is dependent on some strong (or identifying) entity for its identity. Its existence and uniqueness are
         dependent on another entity with which it has an identifying relationship.
         ksjerÈh ÿ¾j, N+;d¾;hla ;ukaf.au m%d:ñl h;=rla iE§ug m%udKj;a .=Kdx. iuQyhla fkdue;s
         w;r tys wkkH;djh i|yd hï m%n, ^fyda y÷kd.ekSfï& N+;d¾:hla u; r|d mj;S' tys meje;au
         iy iqúfYaI;ajh r|d mj;skafka th y÷kd.ekSfï iïnkaO;djhla we;s fjk;a N+;d¾:hla u; h'

     B - A derived attribute is represented as an attribute in a relation.
         jHq;amkak lrk ,o Wm,laIKhla (derived attribute) j.=jla ;=,§ Wm ,laIKhla f,i ksrEmKh
         fõ
         Wrong. In ER modeling, a derived attribute is one whose value is calculated from other attributes
         within the database. For example, an employee's age might be derived from their birthdate. While
         derived attributes are often shown in ER diagrams to illustrate these dependencies or calculations, they
         are generally not stored in relational tables as part of the physical database schema. Instead, their
         values are computed as needed.
         jerÈh. ER wdlD;slrKfha§" jHq;amkak Wm,laIKhla hkq o;a; iuqodh ;=< we;s wfkl=;a .=Kdx.
         j,ska w.h .Kkh lrkq ,nk tlls' ksoiqkla jYfhka" fiajlfhl=f.a jhi Tyqf.a Wmka Èkfhka
         ,nd .; yelsh' fuu mrdh;a;;d fyda .Kkh lsÍï kso¾Ykh lsÍu i|yd jHq;amkak Wm,laIK
         fndfyda úg ER rEm igyka j, fmkajd we;s w;r" tAjd idudkHfhka fN!;sl o;a; iuqod l%ufha
         fldgila f,i iïnkaOl j.= j, .nvd fkdfõ' tA fjkqjg" tAjdfha w.hka wjYH mßÈ .Kkh
         lrkq ,efí'

     C - An entity can contain a multi-value attribute and a composite attribute at the same time.
         jia;=jlg tljr nyq-w.h Wm,laIKhla iy ixhqla; .=Kdx.hla wvx.= úh yel'
         Correct. In ER modeling, an entity can indeed contain both multi-value and composite attributes. A
         multi-value attribute can store multiple values for a single attribute (like multiple phone numbers for
         a contact), while a composite attribute is one that can be subdivided into other attributes (like an
         address composed of street, city, state, and zip code).
         ksjerÈh. ER wdlD;s ks¾udKfha§" N+;d¾:hlg nyq-w.h iy ixhqla; .=Kdx. hk folu wvx.=
         úh yel' nyq-w.h Wm,laIKhg ;ks Wm,laIKhla i|yd nyqúO w.hka .nvd l< yel
         ^iïnkaO;djla i|yd ÿrl:k wxl lsysmhla jeks&" ixhqla; .=Kdx.hla hkq fjk;a Wm,laIK j,g
         fnÈh yels tlls ^ùÈh" k.rh" m%dka;h iy ;eme,a fla;hlska j,ska iukaú; ,smskhla jeks&'

     All statements A and C correctly describe aspects of ER modeling

     Selection f;aÍu

     Repetition mqk¾lrKh

     loop structure.

     If there is a point where an arrow that is moving down comes from a different path and collides with an
     arrowhead, the repetition takes place from that point down.

     Input n

     Is a >= 0 ?

     and outputs r and terminates the process. If the condition is not true then iterate over and over again until

     An input is taken from the user only once.
     mßYS,lhdf.ka tlajrla muKla wdodkhla .kq ,efí'

     Since this is a post-test while loop, the first loop must be executed. where a value is taken as input. The
     value of a has already been replaced by 10 and while moving down it also decreases by 1 in 10. But
     since this value is also greater than 0, the condition must be true and the corresponding output will be

     fuys l%shd;aul jkafka miq mßlaId lsÍfï while Æmhla neúka m<uq Æmh wksjdrfhkau l%shd;aul

     If the number given as input is less than 0, one is added to that number and that number is necessarily
     output. where an output as 9 can never come. Therefore, this statement is false.

     In order to perform the above calculation, it is necessary to have an understanding of the operator
     precedence. The operator precedence is as follows.
     by; .Kkh lsÍu isÿ lsÍugkï fufyhjk m%uqL;d wkqms<sfj< ms<sn|j wjfndaOhla mej;Su

     1.      Parentheses                ()
     2.      Exponentiation             **
     3.      Multiplication             *
             Division                   /
             Integer division           //
             Modulus division           %
     4.      Addition                   +
             Subtraction                -

     tA wkqj by; .Kkh lsÍu my; mßÈ isÿ l, yel'

     (25 % (4)) **3
     1 **3
     1

    pop() method
    This can be used to remove an element at a specific index in the list. Here the poped element is displayed
    on the screen. If desired, it can be assigned to another variable instead of being displayed on the screen.
    ,ehsia;=fõ ksIaÑ; o¾Ylhl we;s wx.hla bj;a lsÍug fuh Ndú; l< yel' fuys§ fmdma jk wx.h

    50

  41. What would be the output of the following Python code?

     for i in range(5):

                      continue
                      val += 2
                else:
                      val += 2

     1) 18                2) 24                 3) 29                4) 38                   5) 39

     Answer : 1 )

     for loop
     Here, a condition depending on a data sequence is introduced to the control variable.
     fuys§ md,k úp,Hhg o;a; wkqms<sfj,la wkqj fldkafoaishla y÷kajd fokq ,efí'
     Included instructions are executed exactly the number of times equivalent to the number of elements in

     .Kkh iudk jdr m%udKhls'
     The common structure of the for loop in Python
     Python ys for loop ys fmdÿ jHqyh
     for variable in (sequence):
            statements

     Methods to obtain the data sequence of the condition
     fldkafoaisfha o;a; wkql%uh ,nd .; yels l%u
     1.   Using a range() function
     2.   Using a string
     3.   Using a tuple
     4.   Using a list

     6.   Using a file

     range() function
     Used to generate a list of integers in a specific range instantly.
     ksIaÑ; mrdihlg wh;a jk ksÅ, ,ehsia;=jla ,nd.ekSu i|yd Ndú; lrhs'
     This can be used in 3 ways.
     fuh wdldr 3lska Ndú;d l< yel'

     range(stop)
     range(start, stop)
     range(start, stop, step)

     The stop value here is always exclusive.
     fuys stop w.h iEu úgu exclusive fõ'
     The start value here is always inclusive. The default of this is 0.
     fuys start w.h iEu úgu inclusive fõ' fuys iïu; w.h 0 fõ'

  47. Which of the following could be used to create an array in PHP?
      PHP ys wrdjla (array) f.dvke.Sug my; ljrla Ndú; l< yels o?
     A - $city[ ] = array("Colombo");
     B - city[ ] = "Colombo";
     C - $city = array("Colombo");

     1) A only.           2) B only.           3) C only.           4) A and C only.       5) B and C only.
        A muKs.              B muKs.              C muKs.              A yd C muKs.           B yd C muKs.

     Answer : 3 )

     Statement A
     $city[ ] = array("Colombo");
     This is incorrect. The correct usage is either to use $city = array("Colombo"); to initialize an array or
     $city[] = "Colombo"; to add an element to an existing array.
     fuh jerÈhs' ksjerÈ Ndú;h $city = array("Colombo"); Ndú;d lsÍuhs' array tlla fyda $city[] =
     "Colombo"; wdrïN lsÍug mj;sk wrdjlg uq,dx.hla tla lsÍu ioyd'

     Statement B
     city[ ] = "Colombo";
     This is incorrect. Variable names in PHP must start with a $ sign. Additionally, this syntax suggests
     assigning a value to an array, which is not a complete array creation statement by itself.
     fuh jerÈhs' PHP ys úp,H kï $ ,l=Klska wdrïN úh hq;=h' óg wu;rj" fuu jdlH LKavh
     uÕska wrdjlg w.hla mejÍug fhdackd lrhs" th iïmQ¾K wrdj ks¾udKh lsÍfï m%ldYhla
     fkdfõ'

     Statement C
     $city = array("Colombo");
     This is correct. This statement creates an array called $city and initializes it with the value "Colombo".
     fuh ksjerÈhs' fuu m%ldYh $city kñka wrdjla ks¾udKh lr th "Colombo" w.fhka wdrïN
     lrhs'

     Therefore, the correct answer is 3).
     tneúka, ksjerÈ ms<s;=r jkafka 3).

     1. Quantum Computing
        lafjdkagï mß.Kkh
         Quantum computing is an advanced area of computing based on the principles of quantum mechanics,
         which is the physics of very small particles like atoms and photons. Unlike classical computers that
         use bits to process information as 0s or 1s, quantum computers use quantum bits, or qubits. Qubits can
         represent and store information as both 0 and 1 simultaneously, thanks to a property called
         superposition.
         lafjdkagï mß.Kkh hkq mrudKq iy fm%dfgdak jeks b;d l=vd wxY=j, fN!;sl úoHdj jk

         folu tljr f;dr;=re ksrEmkh lr .nvd l< yel'
         These properties enable quantum computers to process a vast amount of information simultaneously,
         potentially solving certain complex problems much faster than classical computers.
         fuu .=Kdx. lafjdkagï mß.Klj,g tljr úYd, f;dr;=re m%udKhla ieliSug yelshdj ,nd

     2. Microprocessors laIqø ilik
         Microprocessors are the central processing units (CPUs) of computers, integrated into a single or a
         few integrated circuits (ICs). They execute instructions from programs to perform essential tasks such
         as arithmetic operations, data processing, and system control.
         laIqø ilik hkq mß.Klj, uOHu ieliqï tAll ^CPU& jk w;r th ;ks fyda tAldnoaO mßm:

         Over the decades, microprocessors have seen significant advancements, moving from 8-bit to 16-bit,
         32-bit, and 64-bit architectures, with continually increasing clock speeds and the introduction of
         multicore designs that allow multiple processes to run simultaneously.
         oYl .Kkdjla mqrd" laIqø ilik ie,lsh hq;= ÈhqKqjla oel we;s w;r" 8-bit isg 16-bit 32-bit

     3. Ant colony l=yqUq ckmo
         Ant Colony Optimization (ACO) is a computational algorithm inspired by the foraging behavior of
         real ants. It is used to solve optimization problems, particularly those that can be represented as finding
         good paths through graphs. In ACO, artificial ants simulate the way real ants find the shortest paths to
         food sources by following pheromone trails. ACO is applied to various problems such as the traveling
         salesman problem, network routing, and scheduling, where it iteratively improves solutions by
         balancing exploration of new paths and exploitation of known good paths. Ant Colony Optimization
         (ACO hkq ienE l=yqUqjkaf.a wdydr fiùfï yeisÍfuka wdNdih ,enQ mß.Kl we,af.dß;uhls'

     4. Inference engine wkqudk tkaðu
         An inference engine is a component of artificial intelligence systems that processes input data against
         a knowledge base of rules and facts to draw conclusions or make decisions. It operates by applying
         logical rules to infer new information, using methods such as forward chaining, which starts with
         known facts and infers new ones, and backward chaining, which starts with a goal and works
         backwards to find supporting facts. Inference engines are used in expert systems, decision support
         systems, diagnostic tools, and other AI applications to automate reasoning and provide solutions based
         on the given knowledge base.
         wkqudk tkaðula hkq ks.ukj,g t<öug fyda ;SrK .ekSug kS;s Í;s iy lreKq ms<sn| oekqï

     5. Expert systems úfYaI{ moaO;shl
         An expert system is a type of artificial intelligence (AI) program designed to replicate the decision-
         making ability of a human expert in a specific domain. It operates by using a knowledge base
         consisting of rules and facts, and an inference engine that processes input data to derive conclusions
         or recommendations. When feeding data to an expert system, the information typically consists of
         problem-specific details or queries, similar to what a specialist in that domain would analyze.
         úfYaI{ moaO;shla hkq ksYaÑ; jiul udkj úfYaI{fhl=f.a ;SrK .ekSfï yelshdj m%;sks¾udKh

     2. OTP(One Time Password-OTP)
        tlajrla muKla Ndú; l< yels uqrmohla
         An OTP, or One-Time Password, is a unique, temporary code used to verify a user's identity or
         authorize a transaction. Typically valid for a short period and usable only once, OTPs are a key
         component in two-factor authentication (2FA) systems and other security protocols. When a user logs
         in or performs a sensitive action, an OTP is sent to them via SMS, email, or a dedicated app, which
         they must enter to complete the process. This adds an extra layer of security beyond traditional
         passwords, ensuring that even if a password is compromised, unauthorized access is still prevented.

     There are many type of CSS Selectors. Below are some of them.
     CSS f;aÍï j¾. fndfyduhla ;sfí' tA w;ßka lsysmhla my;ska oelafõ'

     1. Universal Selector (*)
     The universal selector selects all elements on the page.
     universal selector msgqfõ we;s ish¨u uq,dx. f;dard .kS'

     * {color: blue;}

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                  -3-                                            #ictfromabc

     2. Element Selector
     The element selector selects elements based on their tag name.
     Element selector tAjdfha Wiq,k kduh u; mokïj uQ,dx. f;dard .kS'

     p {color: blue;}

     3. Class Selector (.)
     The class selector selects elements with a specific class attribute. It is denoted by a dot (.) followed by
     the class name.
     Class selector úfYaIs; mka;s Wm,laIK iys; uQ,dx. f;dard .kS' th ;s;lska (.) miqj mka;s kduh
     u.ska oelafõ'

     .intro {color: blue;}

     4. ID Selector (#)
     The ID selector selects an element with a specific id attribute. It is denoted by a hash (#) followed by the
     ID name.
     ID selector ksYaÑ; id Wm,laIK iys; uQ,dx.hla f;dard .kS' th yeIa tllska (#) miqj ye÷kqï
     kdufhka ksrEmKh flf¾'

     #header {color: blue;}

     5. Attribute Selector
     The attribute selector selects elements based on the presence or value of their attributes.
     Attribute selector tAjdfha Wm,laIK meje;au fyda w.h u; mokïj uQ,dx. f;dard .kS'

     a[target] {color: blue;}
     a[target="_blank"] {color: green;}

     6. Group Selector
     The group selector selects multiple elements and applies the same styles to all of them.
     Group selector, elements lsysmhla f;dardf.k tA ish,a,gu tlu fudaia;r fhdohs'

     h1, h2, h3 {color: blue;}

     2. Internal CSS
     Internal CSS is used to apply styles within the <style> tag in the <head> section of an HTML document.
     HTML f,aLkhl <head> fldgfia <style> Wiq,kh ;=< fudaia;r fh§ug wNHka;r CSS Ndú;d
     lrhs'

            color: blue;
            text-align: center;

       </style>
     </head>
     <body>
       <p>This is an internally styled paragraph.</p>
     </body>
     </html>

     3. External CSS
     External CSS is used to apply styles by linking an external stylesheet file using the <link> tag in the
     <head> section of an HTML document.
     HTML f,aLkhl <head> fldgfia we;s <link> Wiq,kh Ndú;fhka ndysr stylesheet f.dkqjla
     iïnkaO lsÍfuka ú,di fh§ug ndysr CSS Ndú;d lrhs'

     </head>

     </body>
     </html>

     /* styles.css */
     p {
       color: blue;
       text-align: center;

     Below are the details about the tags we use while creating a form.
     my;ska oelafjkafka fmdaruhla iE§fï§ wm úiska Ndú;d lrk Wiq,khka ms,sno úia;r fõ'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -6-                                            #ictfromabc

     1. Form Tag (<form>):
        <form action="process.php" method="post">
        The <form> tag is used to create an HTML form for user input. The action attribute specifies the URL
        where the form data will be sent when the form is submitted. The method attribute specifies the HTTP
        method (GET or POST) to use when sending form data.
        <form> Wiq,kh mßYS,l wdodkh i|yd HTML fmdaruhla ks¾udKh lsÍug Ndú;d lrhs' action
        Wm,laIKh fmdaruh bÈßm;a lrk úg fmdaru o;a; hjkq ,nk URL tl i|yka lrhs' method
        Wm,laIKh uÕska fmdaru o;a; heùfï§ Ndú;d lsÍug HTTP l%uh (GET fyda POST) i|yka
        lrhs'

     2. Input Text Field (<input type="text">):
        <input type="text" name="name">
        The <input> tag with type="text" creates a single-line text input field. The name attribute specifies the
        name of the input field, which will be sent as a key-value pair when the form is submitted.
        type="text" iys; <input> Wm,laIKh ;ks fma<s mdG wdodk lafIa;%hla ks¾udKh lrhs' name
        Wm,laIKh wdodk lafIa;%fha ku i|yka lrhs" fmdaruh bÈßm;a lrk úg th h;=re-w.h
        hq.,hla f,i hjkq ,efí'

     3. Checkboxes (<input type="checkbox">):
        <input type="checkbox" name="module[]" value="ICT"/>ICT<br>
        The <input> tag with type="checkbox" creates a checkbox input. The name attribute, ending with [],
        allows multiple checkboxes to be grouped together and sent as an array. The value attribute specifies
        the value that will be sent to the server if the checkbox is checked.
        type="checkbox" iys; <input> Wiq,kh msßlaiqï fldgq wdodkhla ks¾udKh lrhs' name >
        Wm,laIKh" [ ] j,ska wjika jk w;r" msßlaiqï fldgq lsysmhla tlg tl;= lr wrdjla f,i
        heùug bv i,ihs' value > Wm,laIKh msßlaiqï fldgqj i,l=Kq l<fyd;a fiajdodhlh fj; hjkq
        ,nk w.h kshu lrhs'

     4. Radio Buttons (<input type="radio">):
        <input type="radio" name="language" value="Sinhala" checked />Sinhala
        The <input> tag with type="radio" creates a radio button input. The name attribute groups radio
        buttons so that only one can be selected at a time. The value attribute specifies the value that will be
        sent to the server if the radio button is selected. The checked attribute pre-selects this radio button.
        type="radio" iys; <input> Wiq,kh f¾äfhda fnd;a;ï wdodkhla ks¾udKh lrhs' name >
        Wm,laIKh f¾äfhda fnd;a;ï iuQy.; lrhs' túg jrlg tlla muKla f;dard.; yel' value
        .=Kdx.h f¾äfhda fnd;a;u f;dard we;akï fiajdodhlh fj; hjkq ,nk w.h kshu lrhs' mÍlaId
        l< Wm,laIKh fuu f¾äfhda fnd;a;u fmr-f;dard .kS'

     5. Drop-down List (<select> and <option>):
        <select name="Center">
        The <select> tag creates a drop-down list. The name attribute specifies the name of the select element.
        <select> Wiq,kh m;k ,ehsia;=jla idohs' name Wm,laIKh select uq,dx.h i|yka lrhs'

        <option value="Colombo" selected>Colombo</option>
        The <option> tag defines an option in the drop-down list. The value attribute specifies the value that
        will be sent to the server if this option is selected. The selected attribute pre-selects this option.
        <option> Wiq,kh m;k ,ehsia;=fõ úl,amhla ks¾jpkh lrhs' value Wm,laIKh fuu úl,amh
        f;dardf.k ;sfí kï fiajdodhlh fj; hjkq ,nk w.h kshu lrhs' f;dard.;a Wm,laIKh fuu
        úl,amh fmr-f;dard .kS'

 (d) The process.php script is invoked after submitting the above form in part (c). After submitting the
     form, it is required to display the name, medium and test center. Complete the following PHP code

     by; (c) fldgfia i|yka wdlD;s m;%h fhduq (submit) lsÍfuka wk;=/j process.php WmfoaYdj,sh

     Answer :
      <?php
      if ($_SERVER["REQUEST_METHOD"] == "POST") {
         $name = $_POST["name"];
         $medium = $_POST["language"];
         $center = $_POST["Center"];}
      echo "<h2> Your Input:</h2>;
      echo $name; echo "<br>";
      echo $medium; echo "<br>";
      echo $center; echo "<br>";

     This PHP script is designed to handle form data submitted via the POST method. When a form is
     submitted, the data is sent to the server, where this PHP script processes it.
     fuu PHP ial%smaÜ ks¾udKh lr we;af;a POST l%uh yryd bÈßm;a lrk ,o fmdaru o;a;
     yeisrùugh' fmdaruhla bÈßm;a l< úg" o;a; fiajdodhlh fj; hjkq ,efí" tys§ fuu PHP
     ial%smaÜ th ilihs'

     1. Checking Request Method:
         if ($_SERVER["REQUEST_METHOD"] == "POST") {
         •   $_SERVER["REQUEST_METHOD"] is a superglobal array that returns the request method
             used to access the page (GET, POST, etc.).

             Ndú;fhka bÈßm;a lr we;s njhs'

      2. Retrieving Form Data:

    Answer :

    for c in S:

    w mode

   Select the most suitable items to replace the labels P and Q from the following list.

   List: { file, functional unit, instruction, memory, page, program counter }

   The fetch-execute cycle, also known as the instruction cycle, is the fundamental operational process of a
   computer's central processing unit (CPU). This cycle describes the steps that the CPU takes to fetch an
   instruction from memory, decode it to determine what action is required, execute that action, and then move
   on to the next instruction. The cycle repeats continuously while the computer is powered on and executing
   programs. Here are the main steps involved in the fetch-execute cycle:
   fijqï wdyrK pl%h" Wmfoia pl%h f,io ye¢kafõ" fuh mß.Klfha uOH ieliqï tAllhl ^CPU&
   uQ,sl fufyhqï l%shdj,sh fõ' fuu pl%h CPU u;lfhka Wmfoia ,nd .ekSug .kakd mshjr úia;r
   lrhs" wjYH l%shdj l=ulaoehs ;SrKh lsÍug th úfla;kh lr" tu l%shdj l%shd;aul lr B<Õ Wmfoia
   fj; hhs' mß.Klh l%shd;aul lr jevigyka l%shd;aul lrk w;r;=r pl%h wLKavj kej; isÿ fõ'
   fijqï wdyrK pl%hg iïnkaO m%Odk mshjr lsysmhls(

   1. The program counter is loaded with the memory address of the relevant instruction of program.
      jevigyka .Klh jevigyfka wod< Wmfoiaj, u;l ,smskh iuÕ mQrKh lrkq ,efí'
       The program counter (PC) holds the address of the next instruction to be executed. At the start of the
       fetch cycle, the PC points to the memory address where the next instruction is located. This address is
       loaded from memory so the CPU knows which instruction to fetch next.

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -21-                                           #ictfromabc

      jevigyka .Klh ^PC& l%shd;aul l< hq;= B<Õ Wmfoia j, ,smskh orhs' ,nd fijqï wdyrK
      pl%h wdrïNfha§" jevigyka .Klh B<Õ Wmfoia we;s u;l ,smskh fj; fhduq lrhs' fuu ,smskh
      u;lfhka mQrKh lr we;s ksid CPU B<Õg ,nd .; hq;= Wmfoia okS'

   2. The instruction is loaded into the instruction register.
      Wmfoia" Wmfoia frðia;rhg mgjkq ,efí'
      The CPU fetches the instruction from the memory location pointed to by the program counter. This
      instruction is then placed into the instruction register (IR), a special register that holds the current
      instruction to be decoded and executed.
      CPU úiska jevigyka .Klh u.ska fmkajd we;s u;l ia:dkfhka Wmfoia ,nd .kS' fuu Wmfoia
      miqj Wmfoia frðia;rfha ^IR& ;ekam;a lrkq ,efí" th úfla;kh lr l%shd;aul lsÍug j;auka
      Wmfoia wvx.= úfYaI frðia;rhla fõ'

   3. The instruction present in the instruction register is decoded.
      Wmfoia f,aLkfha we;s Wmfoia úfla;kh lrkq ,efí'
      The CPU's control unit decodes the instruction in the instruction register. Decoding involves interpreting
      the binary code of the instruction to determine which operation needs to be performed and what operands
      are involved.
      CPU ys md,k tAllh Wmfoia f,aLkfha we;s Wmfoia úfla;kh lrhs' úfla;kh lsÍu hkq l=uk
      fufyhqula isÿ l< hq;=o hkak iy l=uk Tfmrkaâ iïnkaO jkafkao hkak ;SrKh lsÍu i|yd
      Wmfoiaj, oaúuh fla;h mßj¾;kh lsÍuhs'

   4. The control unit of the CPU passes the decoded instruction as a sequence of control signals to the
      relevant functional unit of the CPU.
      CPU ys md,k tAllh CPU ys wod< l%shdldÍ tAllh fj; md,k ix{d wkqms<sfj,la f,i úfla;kh
      lrk ,o Wmfoia ,nd fohs'
      Based on the decoded instruction, the control unit generates a set of control signals that are sent to the
      appropriate functional units within the CPU (such as the arithmetic logic unit (ALU), registers, and other
      components). These signals direct the functional units to perform the required operations (e.g.,
      arithmetic operations, data movement).
      úfla;kh lrk ,o Wmfoia u; mokïj" md,k tAllh CPU ;=< we;s iqÿiq ld¾hnoaO tAll fj;
      hjkq ,nk md,k ix{d iuQyhla ckkh lrhs ^wxl .Ks;uh yd ;d¾lsl tAllh (ALU)" frðia;r
      iy wfkl=;a ixrpl jeks&' fuu ix{d wjYH fufyhqï isÿ lsÍu i|yd ld¾hnoaO tAll fj; fhduq
      lrhs ^Wod( wxl .Ks; fufyhqï" o;a; p,kh&'

   5. The program counter is changed to point to the next instruction.
      jevigyka .Klh B<Õ Wmfoia fj; fhduq lsÍug fjkia lrkq ,efí'
      The program counter is updated to point to the next instruction in the sequence. This usually involves
      incrementing the PC by the size of the instruction just fetched (e.g., incrementing by 1 for a single-byte
      instruction). In cases of branching or jumping, the PC might be updated to a non-sequential address.
      wkqms<sfjf,ys B<Õ Wmfoia fj; fhduq lsÍu i|yd jevigyka .Klh hdj;ald,Sk lrhs'
      idudkHfhka fuhg we;=<;a jkafka oeka ,nd .;a Wmfoiaj, m%udKfhka jevigyka .Klh jeä
      lsÍuhs ^Wod( ;ks nhsÜ Wmfoia i|yd 1 lska jeä lsÍu&' fuys§ jevigyka .Klh wkql%ñl fkdjk
      ,smskhlg hdj;ald,Sk l< yel'
   6. Steps from Step 2 are repeated.
      mshjr 2 isg mshjr kej; kej;;a isÿ flf¾'
      The cycle repeats: the next instruction is fetched, loaded into the instruction register, decoded, and
      executed. This process continues until the program ends or an interrupt occurs.
      pl%h mqkrdj¾;kh fõ( B<Õ Wmfoia ,ndf.k" Wmfoia frðia;rhg mQrKh lr" úfla;kh lr
      l%shd;aul fõ' jevigyk wjika jk ;=re fyda ndOdjla isÿjk f;la fuu l%shdj,sh È.gu mj;S'
